42	   leaves to match: `repo`, `dry_run`, `private`, `account`,
43	   `config`, `use_template` + their consumers
44	   (`init.rs`, tests).

48	   issue.

50	   companion repo + `.vc-config.toml` to an existing por
51	   workspace; emit cross-links going forward. Manual
52	   setup on an external por workspace (2026-05-14)
53	   proved arduous; this should be a routine subcommand.

61	4. **vc-x1 push: `--merge` flag (close-out shape).** Teach
62	   push to set up the non-FF merge ("trapezoid") shape
63	   itself instead of requiring the user to pre-rebase
64	   before invoking push. Sibling to `--squash` (#26);
65	   both are close-out shape choices made at push time.

83	5. **Investigate `linkme` for subcommand registration.**
84	   Distributed-slice registry — each subcommand registers itself
85	   at link time; `main.rs` discovers them via the slice rather
86	   than matching a `Commands` enum. Reduces per-subcommand
87	   touchpoints from 3 (mod decl + enum variant + match arm) to 1
88	   (registration). Costs: loses compile-time exhaustiveness
89	   (missing registration = runtime gap); help-output ordering
90	   depends on link order unless sorted; macro-magic dependency.
91	   Revisit once the `0.50.0` trait sweep's per-arm cost has been
92	   felt under real "add a subcommand" load.
93	   <https://github.com/dtolnay/linkme>
94	6. **Investigate `inventory` as `linkme` alternative.** Same
95	   shape as `linkme` — runtime-iterable registry populated by
96	   `inventory::submit!` per subcommand. Trade-offs mirror
97	   linkme's. Pick one if/when the trait sweep's match becomes
98	   the felt bottleneck.

284	## Bugs
285	
286	   Known defects we're aware of but haven't scheduled a fix
287	   for. Each entry should describe what goes wrong, when,
288	   and the cost of the failure. Numbering convention same as
289	   `## Todo` (manual `1.` `2.` …).
290	
291	1. **`finalize::surface_previous_failures` is racy and
292	   bounded by "next vc-x1 run".** The current model has
293	   four gaps:
294	   - **Stale forever.** Markers sit on disk until the
295	     next `vc-x1 <anything>` runs. If the user abandons
296	     the workspace (e.g., CI / scheduled use), failures
297	     are never surfaced.
298	   - **Concurrent surface_previous_failures.** Two
299	     `vc-x1` runs racing: both `read_dir`, both print
300	     the marker, one deletes (the other's `remove_file`
301	     silently fails). The user sees the same failure
302	     printed twice.
303	   - **Mid-write torn read.** A `finalize --exec` child
304	     writing a marker while a sibling `vc-x1` is
305	     surfacing could read partial content. Atomic-rename
306	     on write would close this.
307	   - **No notify-at-failure path.** A detached
308	     `finalize --exec` failure only becomes visible when
309	     the user next runs *any* `vc-x1` command — fine for
310	     interactive use, invisible for CI / scheduled use
311	     where there may be no next run.
312	
313	   The exec-child gate in `main.rs` (since 0.52.0-3)
314	   patches one related case — the detached child eating
315	   its own prior markers before the user can see them —
316	   but doesn't address any of the above. Holistic fix
317	   needs locking + atomic writes + maybe a
318	   notify-at-failure path for hands-off use.
319

> AGENT

`## Priorities` works but it's fragile in a specific way: the entry numbers it cites (`#2`, `#5`, `#7`) aren't stable — the file's own intro says `vc-x1 fix-todo --no-dry-run` renumbers freely on insert/reorder. The moment you add an entry above #2 or run fix-todo, the priority list silently points at the wrong items.

Two tighter alternatives:

1. **Inline priority marker on the entry itself** — prefix the bold title with `[P0]` / `[P1]` (or `🔴` / `🟡` if you want emoji). Intent lives with the item; survives any renumber; one source of truth. `## Priorities` then disappears, and `validate-todo` could later sort-check that markers come first.

   ```
   2. **[P0] single-field `options_flags` leaves → `value` field.** …
   ```

2. **Keep `## Priorities`, but cite by title not number.** Titles are far more stable than position; readable to a human; survives fix-todo. Costs one line of duplication per priority entry.

   ```
   ## Priorities
   - single-field `options_flags` leaves → `value` field
   - `por → dual` conversion
   - `por/dual` parity + `dual → por` conversion
   ```

I'd lean toward **(1)** — single source of truth, no duplication, naturally extensible to `[P1]`/`[P2]` tiers, and clear from any view of the entry that it's prioritized. The cost is a small convention to remember; the gain is that you can never end up with a stale priorities list.

> DEVELOPER

I like 2, as you don't have to scan/search and the edit it localized as we'll as I can add "new things", like chores-12, also easily and localized. Push back if you disagree

> AGENT

Agreed — the extensibility point flips it. Inline markers can only tier `## Todo` entries; a `## Priorities` list by title can also point at chores sections, bugs, design notes, or anything else that's "important right now," which is a different (and more useful) concept than P0/P1 tiers on Todo alone.

One small thing to be aware of: titles can drift if you rename a `## Todo` entry's bold title and forget to update `## Priorities`. Low risk in practice (titles are stable, and you'd notice), and `vc-x1 validate-todo` could later add an exact-match check ("every `## Priorities` line that isn't a chores/bugs ref matches some Todo entry's title") to make it tooling-enforced.

Want me to clean up the current `## Priorities` content — rewrite the three lines to match the actual entry titles (the current `#2 single-field`, `#5 por -> dual`, `#7 por/duality parity` are abbreviated and the numbers will go stale)?

> AGENT

Both ideas land well. Three small refinements:

1. **Two dots vs three.** `..` reads like a Rust range or a typo. The standard ellipsis is `...` (three dots, easy to type) or `…` (single char, matches the em-dashes used elsewhere). Pick one and stick with it — I'd lean `...` for typeability.

2. **Trailing space inside the bold.** `**single-field.. **` has a space before the closing `**`. Cleaner as `**single-field...**`. Markdown tolerates the space but it's slightly off.

3. **Multiple items at the same tier — one per line.** Currently `P3: **por -> dual...** & **por/duality parity...**` joins two with `&`. Splitting to two `P3:` lines is more grep-friendly (`grep '^P3:'` gives you all of them) and reads the same whether a tier has 1 or 5 items:

   ```
   P3: **por -> dual...**
   P3: **por/duality parity...**
   ```

Worth adding a one-line convention note above the section so the rules are self-describing:

```
## Priorities

 Prefix-match `## Todo` titles with `...` (e.g. `**single-field...**` →
 unique starts-with). Lines that don't prefix-match are free-form
 (chores tasks, ad-hoc reminders). `Pn:` tiers group equal-priority
 items; one item per line.

P1: chores-11 is "full" > 1,000 lines, start chores-12.md
P2: **single-field...**
P3: **por -> dual...**
P3: **por/duality parity...**
```

The semantics ("tier vs strict rank") get pinned down too — `Pn:` is a tier, multiple lines OK. And `vc-x1 validate-todo` could later enforce: any line whose body looks like `**...**` prefix-matches exactly one `## Todo` title.

One question for the spec: should P1 be the **highest** priority (current draft) or **lowest** (some teams use P1=urgent, others P0=urgent)? Worth nailing in the convention note so it doesn't flip later.

> DEVELOPER

P1 = highest, update the convention note and use ...

> AGENT

Three things caught my eye:

1. **Bullet 1 trails off.** "`Pn:` tiers group equal-priority" — needs an object: "...group equal-priority **entries**." Also, the `P1 = highest` note got lost — a reader can't tell if P0 or P1 is the top tier. Add it.

2. **Bullet 2 grammar.** "lines with `...` **is** a prefix-match" — plural subject, should be **are**. And a line doesn't *is* a prefix-match — it *uses* one. Suggest: "a trailing `...` marks a prefix-match against a `## Todo` title (must be unique)".

3. **Bullet 3 double space** between "without" and "a".

Also worth noting (not a bug, a good choice): wrapping the markers in backticks (`` `**single-field...**` ``) renders them as inline code instead of bold, which is what you want — the `**` are *markers* signaling "match this `## Todo` title prefix," not emphasis. If they were actually bolded, the asterisks would vanish in the rendered view and the prefix-match convention would be invisible. The convention note should mention this so it doesn't drift back to plain bold later.

Suggested fix:

```
## Priorities

- Tiered list, highest priority first. `Pn:` groups
  equal-priority entries (P1 is highest); one entry per line.
- A body of `` `**prefix...**` `` (inline-code preserves the
  `**` markers) is a prefix-match against a `## Todo` title —
  must be unique.
- Entries without a `...` prefix-match are free-form
  (chores tasks, ad-hoc reminders).
```

Want me to apply this, or do you want to keep iterating the wording yourself?

> DEVELOPER

I've tweaked differently, but there is another even more immediate problem. todo.md is too large and you're using 1000+ tokens to read the it and you took 30+ seconds, that's a waste of energy. I'm thinking we need to split todo.md refactoring with less important maybe todo.other.md, thoughts?

> AGENT

Two things at once — and the cheaper fix is probably on my side.

**My fault first.** I read the *whole* file when I needed `## In Progress` + `## Priorities` — maybe 30 lines. Read with `offset`/`limit` would have cost a fraction. CLAUDE.md could codify: "to check current focus, read `notes/todo.md` lines 1–50 only; load the rest only when picking up an item." That alone reclaims most of the cost without touching the file structure.

**On the split itself — pushback on the "important vs other" axis.** Two problems:

- **"Important" is fuzzy and migrates.** Today's #3 becomes tomorrow's `todo.other` entry, then back again. Items churn between files as Priorities shifts. Whereas the *current* mechanism — `## Priorities` referencing entries in `## Todo` — already answers "what's important" without moving anything.
- **Numbering domain splits.** `#3` in `todo.md` vs `#3` in `todo.other.md` collide; conversational refs ("let's do #3") need a file qualifier; `fix-todo` has to renumber across both.

**Cleaner split — by lifecycle state, not importance.** Keep `todo.md` as the hot, frequently-changing surface (everything that gets touched per cycle); move the cold backlog to its own file:

- `todo.md` → intro, `## In Progress`, `## Priorities`, `## Bugs`. ~60 lines. Loaded every session.
- `todo-backlog.md` (or `backlog.md`) → the long `## Todo` list. Read only when picking up an item, or via grep.
- `done.md` already follows this pattern.

Why this works:
- The split matches access frequency, not subjective importance.
- Priorities still references backlog entries by `**prefix...**` — no semantic change, just a different file resolves the prefix.
- Numbering stays a single domain (one Todo list, lives in one file).
- `fix-todo` / `validate-todo` get one new file path; small change.

My honest take: **start with the bot-reads-less fix** (cheap, behavioral, zero churn). If the file keeps growing past ~500 lines, then do the lifecycle split. Splitting prematurely adds tooling and cross-file friction for a problem that may not need it yet.

18	## Working Directory
19	
20	Prefer staying in the project root. Use `-R` flags or absolute paths
21	to target other directories rather than `cd`. If `cd` seems necessary,
22	discuss with the user first — losing track of cwd causes subtle
23	command failures downstream.
24	
25	When invoking shell commands, prefer the shortest unambiguous path —
26	usually relative to cwd (`ls notes/`, not
27	`ls /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/`). Long absolute paths
28	clutter the transcript without adding information. Out-of-workspace
29	paths (`/tmp/...`, `~/.config/...`) stay absolute. Read/Edit/Write
30	tool args also stay absolute — that's a tool-boundary constraint,
31	not a stylistic choice.
32	
33	## Memory
34	
35	Do not use the bot's per-project memory directory
36	(`~/.claude/projects/<path>/memory/`). In a dual-repo setup with
37	CLAUDE.md it provides no capability CLAUDE.md doesn't already cover,
38	and it loses on discoverability:
39	
40	- **CLAUDE.md** — at the repo root, a well-known location for bot
41	  instructions, committed, reviewable, visible to every collaborator
42	  (human or bot).
43	- **Memory directory** — hidden under the user's home, tied to one
44	  machine, invisible to anyone but the bot, never diffed or reviewed.
45	
46	Easy for everyone to find beats convenient for the bot alone. Put
47	durable context in CLAUDE.md (or committed `notes/`) instead.
48	
49	## Speculation marker
50	
51	Durable text the bot writes — CLAUDE.md, `notes/`, commit bodies,
52	chores sections — should stick to observations and direct descriptions
53	of the code or data. If a mechanism, hypothesis, or causal claim
54	enters the text, prefix it with "The bot thinks ..." so a reader can
55	tell the measured from the inferred.
56	
57	**Why:** unmarked speculation reads like evidence, and a future reader
58	(or the bot on a later session) can pick it up as a known fact when
59	it's not. Measured / inferred is a distinction worth keeping visible
60	in the written record.
61	
62	**How to apply:** observations and factual descriptions need no
63	marker. Prefix with "The bot thinks ..." (or a close variant like
64	"The bot's guess is ...") when the claim is a mechanism ("X wins
65	because Y caches better"), a cause ("the drift was due to thermal
66	state"), a prediction ("this should scale linearly"), or any
67	reasoning not directly supported by the data on hand.
```

1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	The chores-NN files are numbered in sequence; older ones are closed
9	(chores-09.md and earlier — through the 0.48.x cycles), and the
10	active one ([chores-10.md](chores-10.md), the 0.49.0 cycle onward)
11	also carries the **refactor-tracking** tables (the Context+Params
12	port and the options_flags extraction).
13	
14	App architecture — the CLI-args / `Context`+`Params` split, a
15	generic module map, the subcommand model, and what the
16	Context+Params port and the options_flags extraction *are* (the
17	*live status* is in chores-10.md) — lives at the repo root in
18	[`../ARCHITECTURE.md`](../ARCHITECTURE.md).
19	
20	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
21	database to better manage the information, TBD.
22	
23	Examples chore file:
24	```
25	# Chores-01.md
26	 
27	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
28	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
29	how and why of a particular chore.
30	
31	## Create a binary that lists jj info 
32	
33	This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
34	and using `jj-lib`
35	```
36	
37	## Workflow and conventions
38	
39	Bot-facing workflow, versioning, and code conventions live in
40	[`../CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md). Start there for:
41	
42	- **Versioning during development** — single-step vs multi-step,
43	  `-N` pre-release suffixes, done-marker discipline.
44	- **Code Conventions** — doc comments on every file / fn / method,
45	  `// OK: …` justifications on `unwrap*` calls, ask-on-ambiguity,
46	  stuck detection.
47	- **Commit-Push-Finalize Flow** — two-checkpoint per-step
48	  discipline with hard stop after finalize.
49	
50	## Todo format
51	
52	Todo.md is organized into `## In Progress`, `## Todo`, `## Bugs`,
53	and `## Done` sections; each item is a short description with
54	reference links to more detail.
55	
56	`## Todo` and `## Bugs` entries carry explicit `1.` `2.` …
57	numbers in the source so you can grep, count, and reference
58	them ("let's work on #3"). You don't hand-maintain the
59	numbers — insert, delete, or reorder entries freely, then
60	`vc-x1 fix-todo --no-dry-run` renumbers the list and
61	normalizes continuation-line indent. `vc-x1 fix-todo` alone
62	only previews; `vc-x1 validate-todo` is the read-only check.
63	The `## Done` section keeps `-` bullets — items aren't
64	referenced by number once completed.
65	
66	Reference citations are **double-bracketed** — `[[1]]`, or
67	`[[2]],[[3]]` for several — so the brackets show when rendered
68	(a bare `[1]` renders as just `1`, which doesn't read as a
69	reference). Separate multiple with a comma: `[[2]],[[3]]`, not
70	`[2,3]` (a single ref key — won't resolve) or `[[2]][[3]]`. The
71	`[N]:` definition lines and inline `[text](url)` / `[text](#anchor)`
72	links stay single-bracketed (their brackets already show in
73	source, and the link text reads as a link when rendered).
74	
75	Examples:
76	
77	# Todo
78	- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
79	- Fix bug Y [[1]]
80	
81	# Done
82	- Fixed issue Z [[2]],[[3]]
83	
84	[1]: bugs.md#bug-y
85	[2]: issues.md#issue-z
86	[3]: fixes.md#fix-z
87	
88	## Reference numbering
89	
90	Every note file (`todo.md`, `chores-NN.md`, `done.md`) keeps a
91	file-local `# References` section at the bottom. Reference numbers
92	are scoped to that file — `[1]` in `chores-07.md` and `[1]` in
93	`chores-01.md` are independent slots that may point at completely
94	different URLs. New chores files start their numbering at `[1]`.
95	
96	Treat `[N]` like a **footnote**: the number is a local slot, only
97	meaningful within its file's `# References`. So a `[N]` *citation*
98	(bare `[N]`, or doubled `[[N]]` — see below) never reuses another
99	file's number; to cite a target a sibling file references, pick
100	your own next-local slot and define it (the same target may carry
101	a different number in each file). A `[N]` *inside a code span*
102	(`` `[72]` ``) is different — that's a quoted identifier: literal
103	text naming a ref-key (often from another file's namespace), data,
104	not a citation; it needs no definition here. To point at a section
105	of another file from prose, use an inline link with an anchor —
106	`[that section](../chores-07.md#…)` — not a bare number.
107	
108	A `chores-NN.md` `# References` entry is usually a
109	`/notes/<file>.md#anchor` (or `/ARCHITECTURE.md`) path, but may
110	also be a **commit reference** —
111	`[N]: <commit-url-with-12-hex-SHA> "<full-40-hex-SHA>"`, cited by
112	a section's `Commits:` line. See
113	[`../CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md#chores-commit-references) for the
114	why and the exact shape.
115	
116	A file's `# References` can be **re-packed** to a contiguous
117	`[1]..[N]` in first-citation-appearance order — walk the file's
118	prose in document order (`todo.md`: `## Todo` then `## Done`;
119	`chores-NN.md`: top to bottom) and number refs as their first
120	`[[N]]` citation appears. This is a file-local rewrite — only
121	that file's `[[N]]` citations and `[N]:` definitions move; every
122	target and sibling file is untouched. A `[[N]]` inside a `` ` ``
123	code span is a literal token, not a citation, and is left alone.
124	Do it opportunistically (when the namespace has drifted enough to
125	annoy), not on a schedule: `todo.md` fragments fastest (entries
126	land and get pruned every cycle) and is the usual candidate;
127	`chores-NN.md` / `done.md` are append-mostly and only need it
128	after an unusual event (e.g. a bulk retrofit that allocated slots
129	out of document order).
130	
131	## Retiring Done entries
132	
133	`todo.md`'s `## Done` section is a rolling buffer of recently shipped
134	work, not a permanent log. Move entries into `done.md` at two natural
135	beats:
136	
137	- **Closing a ladder** — when the final `X.Y.Z` (no suffix) commit
138	  ships, decide which prior entries are no longer needed for nearby
139	  context and migrate them.
140	- **Opening a new ladder** — at `X.Y.Z-0`, do the same sweep before
141	  bumping the version.
142	
143	Migration mechanics:
144	
145	- Move the bullet itself from `todo.md > ## Done` to
146	  `done.md` (preserving the original ref number).
147	- Copy any references the moved entries cite into
148	  `done.md`'s `# References` section (those refs are file-local,
149	  so coexisting with `todo.md`'s namespace is fine).
150	- Prune any references in `todo.md > # References` no longer cited
151	  by anything in `## In Progress` / `## Todo` / `## Done`. This
152	  frees the numbers for future reuse.
153	
```

> AGENT

Agreed on the shape — "succinct problem + bullet plan" is sharper than the current `## Prose form` rule ("short intro then bullets"), which gets stretched into paragraphs in practice. Three thoughts:

1. **What the shape captures.** Problem statement = the *why*, one or two sentences. Plan bullets = the *what/when*. Keeping them separate keeps each honest — the problem doesn't get padded with intent, and the plan can shift without rewriting the problem.

2. **In Progress vs. Todo entry differ only in plan formality.**
   - **In Progress** plan: numbered ladder (`-0`, `-1`, …), `(current)`/`(done)` markers — it's a committed sequence.
   - **Todo entry** plan: rough bullets, no numbering, no markers — it's a sketch that gets formalized only when the entry is picked up. Worth saying explicitly in CLAUDE.md so the two don't get conflated.

3. **Retrofitting existing Todo entries is its own work.** Most current entries are paragraph-style. A sweep to convert them to "problem + bullet plan" is a separate effort — add as a new `## Todo` line in this cycle's `-1` commit, not land in `-1` itself.

Proposed rewrite of In Progress (intro shrinks from ~12 lines to 2):

```
**refactor: notes/todo.md restructure + bugs.md split (0.58.0)**

`notes/todo.md` is too large for routine reads and its
`## Todo` intro duplicates `notes/README.md`.

- 0.58.0-0 Preparation (done)
  - backfill `0.57.0` `Commits:` ref in chores-11
  - bump Cargo.toml to `0.58.0-0`
  - open chores-12 with bare section header
  - add minimal `## File reads` to CLAUDE.md
  - populate `## In Progress` (this block)
- 0.58.0-1 add `## Priorities` + trim `## Todo` intro
- 0.58.0-2 move `## Bugs` → `notes/bugs.md`; pointer in todo.md
- 0.58.0-3 expand CLAUDE.md `## File reads` to bugs.md / chores
- 0.58.0 close-out: move In Progress block into chores-12;
  `## Done` entry; update `notes/README.md`
```

The CLAUDE.md convention update (formalize "problem + bullet plan" + the In-Progress-vs-Todo formality distinction) fits in `-3` or close-out.

Want me to apply the rewrite and proceed?

> AGENT

Shipped. On `todo-backlog.md`:

**Filename.** `todo-backlog.md` reads cleanest — descriptive, no collision with existing files, "backlog" is universally understood as the long-tail list. Alternative `backlog.md` is shorter but loses the `todo-` prefix grouping. I'd go `todo-backlog.md`.

**The interesting design point — references.** todo.md's `# References` is `[1]`–`[33]`. Some refs are cited only by `## Todo` body, some only by `## Done`, some by both. Three approaches:

1. **Per-file re-pack.** Each file (todo.md, todo-backlog.md) gets its own freshly-numbered `# References` section containing only the refs cited by *its* body. Refs cited by both get duplicated (different numbers in each file is fine — per the existing "Reference numbering" convention, numbers are file-local). Cleanest end state; one-shot ref re-pack per file per the README's documented mechanic.

2. **Move-only.** Move every ref to whichever file cites it; for refs cited by both, pick canonical owner and have the other file cite via cross-file link. More mechanical, less duplication, but introduces cross-file ref chains.

3. **Leave canonical in todo.md, point from todo-backlog.md.** Easiest commit but creates a permanent cross-file dependency for ref resolution.

I'd recommend **(1)** — it matches the existing "re-pack opportunistically" convention and leaves each file self-contained. The duplication cost is low (refs are one-liners) and self-containment makes future moves easy.

**Other small decisions:**

- **`## Todo` heading in todo.md** → keep as `_See [todo-backlog.md](todo-backlog.md)._` (parallel to Bugs).
- **Priorities convention** → one-line update: "A line with `...` is a prefix-match against a `## Todo` title **in `todo-backlog.md`**".
- **`vc-x1 fix-todo` / `validate-todo` invocation docs** → one-liner in notes/README.md noting backlog is the file to pass.

**Out-of-scope question worth flagging:** `## Done` in todo.md still holds 0.51.0-0.57.0 entries (~7 lines). Worth extracting too, or leave for a future cycle? I'd leave it — it's small, recent context is genuinely useful at todo.md head, and there's no equivalent "too big" pressure.

Thoughts on the references approach?

> DEVELOPER

Yes 1.

Are you suggesting todo.md only has ## In Progress and ## Priorities and everything else is in todo-backlog?

> AGENT

No — my proposal keeps `## Done` in todo.md. The shape I had in mind:

- **todo.md** (live focus): intro, `## In Progress`, `## Priorities`, `## Todo` (pointer), `## Bugs` (pointer), `## Done` (rolling buffer of recent ships), `# References` (Done-only refs).
- **todo-backlog.md**: intro, `## Todo` (entries), `# References` (Todo-only refs).
- **bugs.md**: already done.
- **done.md**: unchanged; gets entries migrated from `## Done` per the existing rolling-buffer rule.

**Why I'd keep `## Done` in todo.md** — it's *by design* a rolling buffer per `notes/README.md > ## Retiring Done entries`. Recent ship context is genuinely useful next to In Progress/Priorities ("what just landed?" frames "what next?"). Moving it out would erase the rolling-buffer concept and make done.md churn every close-out.

**But it's a real call** — if you want todo.md as a strict "currently-active" surface (In Progress + Priorities + pointers, nothing else), I'd push back, not refuse. The aggressive split saves ~10-15 lines and gives perfect symmetry (every historical/cold surface pointed to a sibling file). Trade-off is losing the at-a-glance recent-ship signal.

Your call on Done. Keep it, or extract it too?

> DEVELOPER

Keeping ## Done in ## Todo feels right. What about the items in ## Priorities?
As an experiment I just made it a bulleted list and added ## The list because
the intro paragraph is bulleted so it was hard to differentiate.

But if we leave the entries in "The List" they need to turn into references.
I'm leaning towards entries in "The List" don't move to todo-backlog but stay
in todo and we use the simple prefix and then for those they are local, and
it means you don't need to waste time and energy reading todo-backlog.

Thoughts?

> AGENT

Your instinct is right — cross-file traversal for routine priority work defeats the goal. Keep prefix-match local.

**Yes to:** Entries that Priorities cites stay in todo.md; non-cited entries live in todo-backlog.md. Prefix-match resolves within todo.md. Bot read of todo.md head sees Priorities + the prioritized entries together.

**Bullet collision is real** — convention intro bullets and tier entries look the same. Two fixes:

1. **Sub-heading approach (your experiment).** `## Priorities` holds the convention; a second heading (`## The list`, `## Active`, `## Now`, etc.) holds the P-lines. Works but adds a heading.
2. **Tier sub-headings.** Keep one `## Priorities` section; group entries under `### P1` / `### P2` / `### P3` sub-headings. Convention bullets stay at top; entries are visually separated by the `###` headings, no extra `##` needed.

I'd lean (2) — one fewer heading, and the P-tier semantics surface in the structure rather than being implicit in line prefixes.

**Naming the prioritized-entries section in todo.md** (whether it's `## The list` or just stays `## Todo`):

- **Keep `## Todo`** — same heading in both files; `vc-x1 fix-todo`/`validate-todo` just work on each. Conversational refs ("work on #3") need file context, but priority context usually implies it.
- **New name** (`## Active`, `## Now`) — semantically clearer but introduces a heading that tools don't recognize. Would need a tooling update or a one-off invocation rule.

I'd lean keep `## Todo`. Same name in two files is fine since file-local tools work file-locally; the small "which file?" friction is acceptable.

**One trade-off to acknowledge:** promotion/demotion = file move (cut from todo.md `## Todo`, paste to todo-backlog.md `## Todo`, run `fix-todo` on both). Small operational cost; OK if priorities shift slowly.

Want to go with: prefix-match stays local; prioritized entries stay in todo.md `## Todo`; non-prioritized to todo-backlog.md `## Todo`; convention vs entries visually separated by tier sub-headings inside `## Priorities`?

> DEVELOPER

yes, tier sub-headings, keep ## Todo

93	3. **Investigate `linkme` for subcommand registration.**
94	   Distributed-slice registry — each subcommand registers itself
95	   at link time; `main.rs` discovers them via the slice rather
96	   than matching a `Commands` enum. Reduces per-subcommand
97	   touchpoints from 3 (mod decl + enum variant + match arm) to 1
98	   (registration). Costs: loses compile-time exhaustiveness
99	   (missing registration = runtime gap); help-output ordering
100	   depends on link order unless sorted; macro-magic dependency.
101	   Revisit once the `0.50.0` trait sweep's per-arm cost has been
102	   felt under real "add a subcommand" load.
103	   <https://github.com/dtolnay/linkme>
104	4. **Investigate `inventory` as `linkme` alternative.** Same
105	   shape as `linkme` — runtime-iterable registry populated by
106	   `inventory::submit!` per subcommand. Trade-offs mirror
107	   linkme's. Pick one if/when the trait sweep's match becomes
108	   the felt bottleneck.

Fix .claude repo history: dev0 through dev2 sessions squashed into wrong commit [[22]],[[23]]\n39. Add `vc-x1 setup` subcommand: completions install, .claude repo init, symlink setup [[24]]\n40. Add dynamic revision completion via `ArgValueCompleter` (jj doesn't complete revsets either) [[25]],[[26]]\n41. Test-tempdir override resolution chain. Both\n    `src/test_helpers::unique_base` and\n    `tests/common/unique_base` currently use\n    `std::env::temp_dir()` (= `$TMPDIR`). Generalize to\n    resolve in priority order: explicit env var (e.g.\n    `VC_X1_TEST_TMPDIR`) → user config\n    (`~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`) → local\n    `.vc-config.toml` → `std::env::temp_dir()` fallback.\n    Useful when a developer wants tests on a tmpfs / SSD /\n    project-local path without exporting `TMPDIR` globally.\n    Open question: do we also expose a CLI parameter\n    (e.g. `vc-x1 --workspace-tmp …`)? Test binaries can't\n    easily accept arbitrary flags via `cargo test --`, so\n    env is the realistic surface for tests; for the\n    `vc-x1` binary itself a flag is feasible but unclear\n    it adds value over the resolution chain.\n42. **`validate-todo` / `fix-todo`: flag malformed lines.**\n    A column-0 line inside `## Todo` / `## Bugs` that is\n    neither an entry (`N. `) nor a heading is malformed;\n    it's currently tolerated silently. > AGENT

0.58.0-4 shipped. Before close-out — a scope call.

The cycle also produced two CLAUDE.md protocol changes worth codifying (we agreed on them in conversation but they aren't in CLAUDE.md yet):

1. **Chores section title-only during cycle, move In Progress block into it at close-out.** Affects `### Preparation` ("Header = provisional commit title; intro that mirrors...") and `### Close-out`.
2. **Problem statement + bullet plan as the shape** for In Progress and chores intros (and informally for Todo entries). Touches `## Prose form`.

Two ways to land these:

- **Option A — one close-out (0.58.0):** Cargo bump + In Progress clear + Done entry + chores-12 finalize + notes/README.md updates + CLAUDE.md protocol codification, all in one commit. Bigger close-out than usual.
- **Option B — add `0.58.0-5` (CLAUDE.md protocol codification), then 0.58.0 close-out (just bookkeeping):** Cleaner per-commit story; keeps close-out true to "bookkeeping only" per the protocol body-shape rule.

I'd lean **B** — the CLAUDE.md protocol changes are substantive content, not bookkeeping. Close-out then stays small and clean.

Either way, the final close-out title needs syncing — current chores-12 header says `refactor: notes/todo.md restructure + bugs.md split (0.58.0)` but the cycle did more (Priorities, todo-backlog split, File reads rule). Proposed: `refactor: notes/todo restructure (0.58.0)` (42 chars; umbrella term that covers everything).

A or B? And OK on the final title?

```
107	- **Post-amend `jj new`** — `jj edit <rev>`,
108	  `jj rebase -r <rev> -d ...`, and `jj squash --into <rev>`
109	  all leave `@` on the rewritten commit rather than on an
110	  empty change above. The most common case is the close-out
111	  push for a [Merge (non-FF)](#pushing) shape (`jj rebase
112	  -r <tip> -d <prev> -d <sub-tip>` leaves `@` on the new
113	  merge). In a colocated jj+git repo, git then detaches
114	  HEAD at the commit's first parent, so `git status`,
115	  gitk, and IDE diff views read the commit's content as
116	  uncommitted relative to HEAD. Follow up with `jj new` to
117	  create an empty `@` above; HEAD shifts to the commit and
118	  the working tree reads clean.
119	
120	## Prose form
121	
122	Long-lived prose on this project follows one shape: a short intro
123	that explains the *why* or the high-level *what*, then a `-` bullet
124	list for the details. Wrap lines at ≤72 cols (bullet continuations
125	indent two spaces). Avoid wall-of-prose paragraphs — they hide the
126	structure that bullets make scannable.
127	
128	Surfaces that use this shape:
129	
130	- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files —
131	  see [Doc comments](#doc-comments-on-every-file-function-and-method).
132	- Commit message bodies (both app-repo and session-repo). The
133	  ≤50-col title is the commit-specific add-on; see
134	  [Per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
135	- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores/chores-NN.md` — see
136	  [Chores section content](#chores-section-content--no-edit-list-git-is-the-record).
137	- Todo and Done entries in `notes/todo.md` when an entry needs more
138	  than one line of detail. Pure one-liners are still fine.
139	
140	Bullet *content* differs by surface:
141	
142	- **Commit bodies** — bullets are file-by-file: one bullet per file
143	  changed, file plus a one-line gist (e.g.
144	  `README.md: new Overview intro`). Source of truth for the
145	  mechanical edit list.
146	- **Chores / todo / done** — bullets are conceptual (design points,
147	  structural notes, the "what landed and why" at a notch above
148	  file-list granularity). Never a copy of the commit's edit list —
149	  see [Chores section content](#chores-section-content--no-edit-list-git-is-the-record).
150	- **Doc comments** — bullets are whatever structure fits (fields,
151	  cases, invariants).
152	
153	**Semicolons inside bullets.** A bullet that joins
154	multiple clauses with semicolons (`A; B; C`) is a list
155	hiding inside running prose — break the clauses into
156	sub-bullets so the structure shows. Semicolons in running
```

```
290	Close-out. One protocol; push and squash are actions you take
291	when wanted, not modes chosen up front. The mechanics below
292	run in the order they happen.
293	
294	### Numbering
295	
296	The version suffix on each commit carries its phase, with one
297	separator — `.` — at every depth (semver mandates `-` once at
298	the start of the prerelease, then `.` for each level after):
299	
300	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
301	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
302	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
303	
304	A **trailing `0` identifier marks a Preparation**, and the
305	rule nests to any depth: when a Work commit needs subdividing
306	into its own sub-cycle, append another level — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
307	(Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle), `X.Y.Z-3.1` /
308	`X.Y.Z-3.2` (its Work), `X.Y.Z-3` (its Close-out),
309	`X.Y.Z-3.1.0` (Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle), and
310	so on. A sub-cycle needing no Preparation just omits the `.0`
311	(`-3.1`, `-3.2`, `-3`); one that grows a Preparation later
312	adds `-3.0` with no renumbering of siblings.
313	
314	Each commit carries its phase version in its title **and**
315	`Cargo.toml` — bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase,
316	so `vc-x1 -V` shows the active phase at build time.
317	
318	**Ordering caveat.** The one ordering guarantee semver gives
319	is that the bare `X.Y.Z` close-out outranks every `X.Y.Z-…`
320	dev version. Within the ladder it does *not* hold for nested
321	close-outs — `X.Y.Z-3` sorts *before* its children
322	`X.Y.Z-3.0` / `X.Y.Z-3.1`, because a prefix always precedes
323	its extensions. Cosmetic — nothing depends on within-ladder
324	order — but don't claim monotonicity.
325	
326	**Why numeric suffixes** (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:
327	semver pre-release identifiers compare per spec and Cargo
328	accepts them. `-dev` adds no information the git log doesn't
329	carry and doubles typing per commit. The final close-out
330	commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) signals completion rather than amending
331	prior commits — history stays readable, exploratory vs final
332	commits obvious.
333	
334	### Preparation
335	
336	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):
337	
338	- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores `Commits:` ref.**
339	  Grab the just-pushed commit's URL + full SHA, fill in the
340	  newest chores section that was left `Commits:`-less. See
341	  [Chores commit references](#chores-commit-references).
342	- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.
343	- **Pick up a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one). Move
344	  that entry's text out of `## Todo` and into `## In
345	  Progress` — its overview and to-do list, followed by the
346	  plan ladder — so `## In Progress` is self-describing, not
347	  a bare ladder. A cycle that merges several entangled
348	  `## Todo` items moves all of them. A `## Todo` entry that
349	  duplicates current `## In Progress` work is a process bug.
350	- **Open the chores section.** Header = provisional commit
351	  title; intro that **mirrors** the `## In Progress` content
352	  so the cycle stays readable once `## In Progress` is
353	  cleared at close-out; any `###` design subsections. No
354	  per-file edit list — that lives in the commit body.
355	
356	### Per-commit flow
357	
358	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
359	through:
360	
361	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
362	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`, before any code/doc
363	   work.
364	2. **Do the work.**
365	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
366	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit, so
367	   the commit captures the completed state.
368	4. **Cargo cycle** (skip-able for purely-docs commits; full
369	   cycle mandatory at close-out):
370	   1. `cargo fmt`
371	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
372	   3. `cargo test`
373	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
374	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
375	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
376	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
377	   working-copy changes in their editor. Most change
378	   requests come here — iterate until the work is right.
379	6. **Write the commit description.** [Conventional
```

474	### Close-out
475	
476	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`):
477	
478	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
479	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
480	- **Finalize the chores section.** Sync the section header
481	  to the **final** commit title and update every markdown
482	  anchor back-reference to it; add an `### As-built ladder`
483	  listing the cycle's commits; `### Outcome` notes if
484	  useful.
485	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
486	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
487	
488	The close-out commit's body should describe **only what's
489	in that commit** (the bookkeeping: clearing `## In
490	Progress`, the `## Done` entry, chores section finalize)
491	— not what would happen post-squash. Squash / merge /
492	keep is a separate push-time decision (Post close-out
493	review, see [Pushing](#pushing) below); keeping the
494	close-out body shape-neutral avoids locking in
495	squash-language before the decision is made.
496	
497	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
498	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
499	see [Pushing](#pushing).
500	
501	### Pushing
502	
503	**Push is discretionary** — done when you want to back up
504	work or publish interim progress, and **always at
505	close-out** (the cycle's result must be published). Interim
506	pushes are a judgment call; nothing forces push-per-commit.
507	
508	**Post close-out review (close-out push only).** After
```

**0.58.0-5 — Commit Description review.**

Title (50 chars):

```
docs: codify cycle protocol updates (0.58.0-5)
```

Body:

```
Three conventions iterated during this cycle land in CLAUDE.md
so future cycles follow the same shape from Preparation onward.

- CLAUDE.md
  -- `### Preparation`: chores section opens with the
     anticipated close-out title as the `##` header, no body;
     `## In Progress` carries the live narrative; one chores
     section per cycle is the default
  -- `### Preparation`: `## Todo` pickup spec sharpened —
     bold title line (exact match for chores header) +
     succinct problem statement + plan ladder
  -- `### Close-out`: new "move `## In Progress` block into
     chores section" step — cut the body, drop the bold
     title line (chores `##` carries it), paste under the
     bare header
  -- `## Prose form`: new "Problem + plan shape" callout —
     `## In Progress`, chores intros, and `## Todo` entries
     use a succinct-problem + plan-bullets shape; ladder
     formality differs (In Progress / chores numbered,
     Todo entries informal)
- notes/todo.md `## In Progress`: mark 0.58.0-5 (done)

ochid: /.claude/pxqmtusnorrp
```

One design call I made without explicit confirmation: at close-out, the bold title line of the In Progress block gets **dropped** when moving into chores (since the chores `##` header carries the same string). The alternative is keeping it as the section's first line (redundant but mirrors the in-progress look). I went with drop — flag if you'd rather keep it.

OK to commit?

> DEVELOPER

Cycle landed as 7 commits on top of `0.57.0`:

- `0.58.0-0` Preparation
- `0.58.0-1` Priorities + intro trim
- `0.58.0-2` bugs.md split
- `0.58.0-3` todo-backlog.md split

- `0.58.0-5` CLAUDE.md protocol codification
- `0.58.0` close-out (bare)

**Shape choices for the publishing push:**

- **Squash** — `main` gets one commit (`refactor: notes/todo restructure (0.58.0)`). Per-step granularity preserved only in the chores `### As-built ladder`. Cost: loses per-commit git history; `git log --grep="todo-backlog"` would only find the squashed commit, not the individual split commit.
- **Merge (non-FF)** — `main` gains one merge commit (`0.58.0`) with parents `<0.57.0>` and the cycle tip `0.58.0-5`; sub-steps stay reachable via the merge's second parent. `git log --first-parent main` reads one jump from `0.57.0` → `0.58.0`. **Same shape as 0.57.0** (recent precedent).
- **Keep separate** — 7 commits land directly on main. Most history; noisiest.

I'd lean **Merge** — the per-step commits are each cohesive (Priorities, bugs split, backlog split, File reads rule, protocol codification) and worth preserving for archaeology; the merge keeps `main --first-parent` reading as one cycle per jump.

Push target: bookmark `main` (the app-repo working branch).

Your choice on shape, and approval to push?

> DEVELOPER

One one hand I like the merge as you can identify the span of a "multi-step" in gitk and `jj log -r ..@ -n 20` but that implies we're going to make it the default from now on, thoughts on that?

> AGENT

Yes, I'd make merge the default — with one carve-out — based on recent practice and the asymmetric tradeoffs.

**Why merge as default works:**

- **Recent practice already converges.** 0.56.0, 0.57.0, and now 0.58.0 all chose merge. Three in a row → the bot is defaulting in conversation; codifying it just makes the default explicit.
- **Merge is the only shape that gives both archaeology AND linearity.** Squash loses sub-step commits (only the As-built ladder remains as evidence). Keep loses linear `main`. Merge keeps both — `git log --first-parent main` reads one jump per cycle; sub-steps reachable via second parent.
- **The "find when X landed" use case wins.** `git log --grep="todo-backlog"` finds the exact `0.58.0-3` commit in merge/keep mode. Under squash it only finds the umbrella commit and you read the body to confirm.

**The carve-out: squash for genuinely-one-logical-change cycles.** A multi-commit cycle whose sub-steps are *intermediate validation points* (not distinct landings) is better squashed — preserves linearity over noise. Example: a refactor that compiles cleanly only at the end, with `-1`/`-2` being intermediate states. The bot should still recommend squash for those.

**What this means for the protocol:**

- Default for multi-commit cycles → merge.
- Squash → explicit opt-in when sub-steps are validation points, not landings.
- Single-commit cycles → the choice is moot (merge of one parent is just the commit).
- Post close-out review still surfaces the choice — but the bot's recommendation default flips from "ask" to "merge unless …".

CLAUDE.md `### Pushing` would gain a one-paragraph default-guidance update. That's a small change, but **doesn't fit in this cycle** (we're at close-out). Two options:

1. **Add to next cycle's Todo** (a new entry to codify "merge as default" in CLAUDE.md `### Pushing`).
2. **One-shot tack-on**: amend the close-out commit (or open a tiny `0.58.1`) — but that's churn for a small text change.

I'd go (1) — natural fit for a fresh cycle. For *this* push, decision still stands: merge?

vc-x1 0.58.0

Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.

Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a
single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a
state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can
resume without re-doing completed stages.

Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)
→ message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →
commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both
→ push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /
commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via
`jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.
After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
recovery is forward-only.

Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes
to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body
across resumes so only the first invocation needs them.

Usage: vc-x1 push [OPTIONS] [BOOKMARK]

Arguments:
  [BOOKMARK]
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (positional form of `--bookmark`).
          
          Accepting a positional lets the common case read as `vc-x1 push main`
          without the `--bookmark` ceremony; `--bookmark` is kept as an alias
          for scripts and for composition with other args. The two forms
          conflict if both supplied.

Options:
      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (flag form; see positional)

  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
          
          Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after printing): the
          banner now rides along with normal subcommand execution rather than
          gating it, so scripts can capture the version *and* the command's
          output in one invocation.

      --restart
          Clear any saved state file and start from stage 1

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --from <STAGE>
          Explicit stage to jump to (advanced / debug use)

          Possible values:
          - preflight:       Run fmt / clippy / test / install / retest
          - review:          Present diff for the first approval gate
          - message:         Compose / edit the commit message; present for
            second gate
```

          - commit-app:      Commit the app repo
          - commit-claude:   Commit the `.claude` session repo (skipped if
            empty)
          - bookmark-both:   Advance both bookmarks to `@-`
          - push-app:        `jj git push --bookmark <b> -R .`
          - finalize-claude: `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <b>
            ...`

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

      --step
          Pause between every stage for an interactive approval gate

      --status
          Print where the saved state thinks we are and exit

      --recheck
          Re-run `preflight` even on resume (default: skip if last run
          succeeded)

      --no-finalize
          Stop before `finalize-claude` so it can be run manually

      --dry-run
          Print the exact commands for every stage without side effects

      --title <STR>
          Commit title (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

      --body <STR>
          Commit body (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

  -y, --yes
          Auto-approve interactive prompts (review gate, message-edit
          confirmation). Required when `--title` / `--body` aren't both supplied
          in non-interactive contexts

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
```

```
fn stage_commit_app(
    root: &Path,
    state: &PushState,
    params: &PushParams,
) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let (title, body) = resolve_message(state, params)?;
    let claude_chid = state
        .claude_chid
        .as_deref()
        .ok_or("push commit-app: claude_chid not set (message stage didn't run)")?;
    let body_with_trailer = format!("{body}\n\nochid: /.claude/{claude_chid}");
    let app_arg = root.to_string_lossy();
    if params.dry_run {
        info!(
            "push commit-app: [dry-run] would run jj commit -R {app_arg} -m \"{title}\" -m <body+ochid>"
        );
        return Ok(());
    }
    info!("push commit-app: jj commit -R {app_arg}");
    run(
        "jj",
        &[
            "commit",
            "-R",
            &app_arg,
            "-m",
            &title,
            "-m",
            &body_with_trailer,
        ],
        root,
    )?;
    Ok(())
}

/// Pull the title/body pair from CLI args or persisted state,
/// preferring CLI args (override-on-resume case). Returns `Err`
/// when neither source has them — which only happens if
/// `stage_message` didn't run or was force-bypassed.
fn resolve_message(
    state: &PushState,
    params: &PushParams,
) -> Result<(String, String), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let title = params
        .title
        .clone()
        .or_else(|| state.title.clone())
        .ok_or("push: title missing — message stage didn't run")?;
    let body = params
        .body
        .clone()
        .or_else(|| state.body.clone())
        .ok_or("push: body missing — message stage didn't run")?;
    Ok((title, body))
}

) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    if !state.claude_had_changes.unwrap_or(false) {

> AGENT

You're right — I was wrong. Source confirms: `stage_commit_app` at `src/push.rs:1096` runs `jj commit` *unconditionally*, no skip-if-empty check. So with empty `@`, it would create a phantom empty code commit on top of `qz` (the merge) with the `--title`/`--body` message + an ochid trailer. That's a bug-shaped outcome — not what we want.

This is a sharper instance of "vc-x1 push doesn't handle the new cycle protocol":

- Old model: WC has changes → push commits both sides 1:1 with shared message → finalize.
- New model: code is fully committed *before* push (the cycle ladder); push only needs to advance bookmarks + push app + finalize .claude. There's nothing to commit on either side (after we pre-commit .claude).

**Refined plan:** skip the commit stages entirely with `--from bookmark-both`:

1. Pre-commit `.claude` manually with the proper title + body + 7 ochid lines (`jj commit -R .claude -m "..." -m "..."`).
2. `vc-x1 push main --from bookmark-both --yes` — runs bookmark-both → push-app → finalize-claude only. No preflight (we just ran it at close-out), no review (we did the Post close-out review), no message (none needed), no commit-app phantom, no commit-claude (would've been skipped anyway).
3. Stop after finalize launches.

**Backlog Todo to add** (high-value since it blocks clean dogfooding of the cycle protocol):

> **`vc-x1 push`: support new cycle protocol shape (1:N code↔bot).** Today `vc-x1 push` assumes 1:1 symmetric WC commits with shared title/body. With the new cycle protocol the code side is fully committed before push (N commits via merge); the `.claude` side needs one commit with its own message + multi-line `ochid:` listing all N code commits. Teach push to: detect this shape; skip commit-app when code WC is empty; compose a `.claude`-specific message (distinct from any code commit); emit multi-line ochid per the design in `notes/forks-multi-user.md`.

> AGENT

Real gap. The follow-ups surface in conversation (and the `.claude` commit body), but neither survives `/exit` in an actionable form — the next session sees them as historical narrative, not as a Todo to act on.

Let me lay out the options and lean on one.

**Option A — your idea, append to chores-XX.md (`### Follow-ups` subsection).**
- Pro: co-located with the cycle that generated them; natural provenance.
- Con: muddies cycle record (what happened) with forward planning (what's next); next session needs the convention "look at the latest chores section's `### Follow-ups` at session start"; section grows stale after picked up.

**Option B — your idea, `notes/next-cycle.md`.**
- Pro: single, well-known location; easy to read at session start.
- Con: new file with overlapping purpose to `todo-backlog.md` ("stuff to do"); rule needed for "when goes in next-cycle vs backlog".

**Option C — write straight to `notes/todo-backlog.md` (or `notes/todo.md > ## Todo` if prioritized) AS PART OF close-out.**
- Pro: uses existing infrastructure — backlog already IS the queue of "stuff to do." No new file, no new section, no new convention. Survives `/exit` naturally because backlog is in the routine acquaint reads. Provenance captured in the close-out commit body ("Backlog additions: #N, #M") and optionally in the entry text ("Surfaced during 0.58.0 close-out.").
- Con: requires extending close-out scope from "bookkeeping only" to "bookkeeping + follow-up capture" — CLAUDE.md needs to say so.

**I'd lean (C).** The backlog is the right home for "stuff to do later." Carry-in items aren't a different category from regular Todos; they're just new entries. The "which cycle surfaced this?" question is answered by the close-out commit body (the commit history is canonical, the Todo entry doesn't need to repeat it).

What changes:
- **CLAUDE.md `### Close-out`** gains a step: "Add any follow-up Todos surfaced by the cycle to `notes/todo-backlog.md` (or `notes/todo.md > ## Todo` if prioritized). List them in the close-out commit body."
- **CLAUDE.md `## Cycle Protocol`** picks up the meta-note: "Close-out often surfaces follow-up items — capture them at close-out so they survive `/exit`."

**For THIS case** (0.58.0 already shipped, follow-ups not captured): two paths to recover —
1. Open 0.59.0 right now in this session, do `-0` prep work (add the Todos to backlog + CLAUDE.md note). Captures the follow-ups before `/exit`. Dogfoods the new technique.
2. Add Todos to backlog as a free-form edit (no cycle ceremony) — but every change is supposed to be a cycle, so this breaks the rule.

I'd go (1) — open 0.59.0 now. Even though it kicks the "fresh-session-per-cycle" pattern down by one cycle, that's the cost of recovering the gap. Going forward, the (C) technique prevents this from happening again.

> AGENT

Two different files came up. Let me confirm what's where before action:

**`er ─────`** (your `er ────`):
- NOT in WC.
- NOT in current HEAD (`git ls-tree -r HEAD` has no match).
- Exists in **orphan commit** `31cae98` (1718 lines, no branch contains it — never pushed, will be gc'd eventually).
- **Effectively a non-issue** — no cleanup needed unless you want to forcibly clear the orphan.

**`close-out.txt`** (142 bytes):
- IS in WC.
- IS in current HEAD (added in our `0.58.0-0` Preparation commit `5d15888`).
- IS pushed to main.
- Content: a single sentence I wrote in conversation during this session, looks like a copy-paste scratch note that got swept into our first commit.

So the actual cruft to clean up is `close-out.txt`, not `er ─────`. (Were you thinking of close-out.txt, or did `er ─────` mean a third file I'm not seeing?)

**Cleanup options for close-out.txt:**

A. **New commit on top removes it** (`jj commit -m "chore: remove stray close-out.txt"`). Non-destructive — file lives in history but gone from current tip. Folds naturally into 0.59.0-0 Preparation since we're about to commit one.
B. **Rewrite history** to remove close-out.txt from every commit it appears in (-0 onward through the merge). Force-push main. Destructive, rewrites pushed commits.

Force-pushing main is highly destructive — even on a solo repo, it rewrites published history.

I'd lean **A**. Cleanup folds into the 0.59.0-0 Preparation commit (one more bullet in its body); close-out.txt becomes a one-commit-lived artifact visible only via `git log -p close-out.txt`.

> DEVELOPER

Overall looks good, but questsion;  the "last" Merge (non-FF) commit, for instance 0.58.0 has two parents the "first" (0.57.0) and the "penultimate" commit (0.58.0.5), what is the diff shown by gitk, is it between "last" and "first" or "last" and "penultimate" or something else?

> AGENT

Good question, and there's a subtlety worth flagging. Three behaviors are in play:

**For merge commits, the default `git`/`gitk` view is the COMBINED diff** (also written `--cc`) — it shows only hunks where the merge's content differs from BOTH parents in an "interesting" way (i.e., what the merger had to do beyond auto-merge). For a clean non-FF merge where the merge commit's content equals the topic-branch tip, the combined diff is typically minimal or empty.

**For our merge (0.58.0 = qz)**:
- First parent = 0.57.0 (mm)
- Second parent = 0.58.0-5 (ly)
- Merge's content = the close-out content (cargo bump to 0.58.0, In Progress cleared, Done entry, chores-12 finalize, README.md updates)

Three diffs you could ask for:

1. **Combined (gitk default for merges):** Likely sparse/empty — merge content is essentially "close-out applied on top of -5", which is clean. You'd see almost nothing.
2. **`git diff 0.58.0^1 0.58.0` (vs first parent = 0.57.0):** The full cycle's accumulated changes — everything from -0 through close-out. This is the "headline" diff and what `git log -p --first-parent main` shows per cycle. **Probably what you want.**
3. **`git diff 0.58.0^2 0.58.0` (vs second parent = 0.58.0-5):** Just the close-out bookkeeping (~5 files). Tiny.

**For gitk specifically:** I'm not 100% sure of its default — I *believe* it shows the combined diff for merges (matching `git show`), and you can right-click to choose "Diff this → first parent" or similar to see (2) or (3) explicitly. Worth verifying by clicking around on the 0.58.0 commit in gitk.

If the combined diff in gitk is empty/minimal, that's why — it's not gitk being wrong, it's just gitk showing the *merge-introduced* changes (none), not the *cycle's accumulated* changes. The accumulated diff lives in `^1`.

To verify behavior: open gitk on the 0.58.0 commit and check what it shows; then right-click and try the parent-specific diff options.

Want to proceed with the commit (the question doesn't change anything about it), or hold while you check gitk first?

> AGENT

Right — `0.58.0` isn't a git ref in this repo. Version strings live in commit titles and `Cargo.toml`, but we don't `git tag` them. So git tries to interpret `0.58.0` as a ref and fails with "ambiguous argument".

Working alternatives:
- **SHA / short prefix** — `git diff a199d06^1 a199d06` (what you used; works).
- **By commit message** — `git log -p --first-parent --grep='(0.58.0)' main` shows the merge commit with diff vs first parent (combined diff by default; pass `-m` or `--first-parent` style to control).
- **Pseudo-ref hack** — `git log --grep='(0.58.0)' -1 --format=%H` extracts the SHA programmatically.

Your output matches the expectation: diff between `0.57.0` (first parent) and the `0.58.0` merge = the whole cycle's accumulated changes. That's the "headline" view of a cycle in the new merge-default shape.

> DEVELOPER

sprinkingly tags at every no-FF doesn't feel right, what'd be nice is `vc-x1 0.58.0..0.57.0` or some such. Of course that is dependent on our naming technique, but would be the "easiest" for regular vc-x1 users. Another is "@" syntax or jj chid's. And finally branch or tag names the user decides on, ATM 0.58.0 == main so `vc-x1 main^1 main` should definitely be supported. Other suggestions?

> AGENT

Agreed — auto-tagging clutters the tag namespace fast (one tag per cycle = lots of tags). A vc-x1 resolution layer is better.

A few angles to weigh in addition to yours:

1. **`vc-x1 sha <ref>` primitive** — single subcommand that resolves any of:
   - Version strings: `0.58.0`, `0.58.0-3`, `0.58.0-3.1`
   - Standard git refs: branches, tags, SHAs (passes through)
   - jj refs: `@`, `@-`, change IDs (passes through)
   - Output: full SHA on stdout
   - Composable: `git diff $(vc-x1 sha 0.58.0)^1 $(vc-x1 sha 0.58.0)`

   Builds the version-string-to-SHA bridge as a primitive, then everything else composes on top.

2. **`vc-x1 diff` / `vc-x1 log` thin wrappers** — accept the version-string vocabulary and dispatch to git. So:
   - `vc-x1 diff 0.57.0..0.58.0` → `git diff $(...)..$(...)`
   - `vc-x1 log 0.55.0..0.58.0` → `git log $(...)..$(...)`
   - `vc-x1 diff main^1 main` → falls through to `git diff main^1 main`

3. **Cycle-relative aliases** — handy conversational shortcuts:
   - `prev` = previous cycle's close-out on `main --first-parent`
   - `cur` = current main tip (latest close-out)
   - `prev~1c` / `prev~2c` etc. — N cycles back along --first-parent

   So `vc-x1 diff prev..cur` always works regardless of version numbering.

4. **`--first-parent` awareness** — for merge-shape cycles, version-string refs should resolve to the merge commit (the bare `X.Y.Z`), not the sub-tip. Then `0.58.0^1` naturally walks first-parent (to `0.57.0`), which is usually what you want when asking "what did this cycle do?"

5. **Worth noting:** `vc-x1 main^1 main` is **free** if vc-x1 accepts standard git refs as pass-through (option 2). `main` is just a git ref; `^1` is git revision syntax. The "main case" needs no special support — only the version-string case does.

Honest scope take: option (1) — the `vc-x1 sha` primitive — is the smallest valuable thing. It unlocks every composition. Options 2-4 are sugar that can come later as users feel the need.

**Backlog Todo material?** Bundled as one entry: "vc-x1 version-string ref resolution (`vc-x1 sha <ref>` primitive + `diff`/`log` wrappers + cycle-relative aliases)." Could land as separate cycles depending on appetite.

add to backlog as one entry, but obviously `vc-x1 chid <ref>` (I assume <ref> == <revision>?) should work too, which we already have so is there a `jj diff $(vc-x1 chid @)^1 $(vc-x1 chid @` where @ is the "correct" revset that would produce the same outout as `git diff a199d062ff6e88b5e2d87d57551d1c60e75b073b^1 a199d062ff6e88b5e2d87d57551d1c60e75b073b`?

> AGENT

Two parts to your question — let me take them in order.

**`vc-x1 chid` already exists** — yes. Per the help (I've been using it all session), `<ref>` is a jj revset (`@`, `@-`, change-id prefix, `main`, etc.). So `vc-x1 chid @-` resolves to the chid of the parent of @ — which is exactly the merge `0.58.0` right now (since @ is the empty 0.59.0-0 WC above the merge).

**But `jj diff $(...)^1 $(...)` won't work as written** — jj's diff syntax isn't `^1`; it uses `--from` / `--to`. Also, `^1` (first parent) in jj revsets isn't a direct operator the way git has it. `X-` returns the *set* of parents, which for a merge is multiple.

**Concrete equivalent** for "the cycle's accumulated diff":

```
jj diff --from $(vc-x1 chid mm) --to $(vc-x1 chid qz)
```

Where `mm` = 0.57.0's chid and `qz` = 0.58.0's chid. With our setup right now:

```
jj diff --from mm --to qz -R .
```

(chids are unambiguous prefixes, so the short forms work)

For an `@`-relative version: `jj diff --from <first-parent-of-@-> --to @-` — but jj 0.40's revset language doesn't have a clean "first parent" operator, so you'd have to name the first parent explicitly. That's a separate gap (jj-side, not vc-x1-side).

**Adding the backlog entry now.** Heads-up: adding new work means we restart the Work review loop — I'll add the entry, then surface for review before going to commit description.

The subsections would be:

```
### Squash carve-out (for CLAUDE.md `### Pushing`)

Default = merge for any multi-commit cycle. Squash = explicit
opt-in for cycles whose sub-steps are intermediate validation
points rather than distinct landings. Every cycle is ≥3
commits (Preparation + ≥1 Work + Close-out), so the
"merge of one parent" edge case is moot.

### Close-out follow-up capture (for CLAUDE.md `### Close-out`)

At close-out, write any follow-up Todos surfaced during the
cycle to `notes/todo.md > ## Todo` (if prioritized) or
`notes/todo-backlog.md` (if long-tail). List them in the
close-out commit body so the additions are reviewable.

### Close-out surfaces follow-ups (meta-note, for `## Cycle Protocol`)

Close-out often surfaces follow-up items that became visible
only by doing the cycle's work. Capture them at close-out
(per the rule above) so they survive `/exit` between cycles —
sessions don't carry conversational context across `/exit`.
```

Two ways to land:
- **As another small commit on top of -0** (call it `-0.1` per nested numbering, or just an unconventional second prep commit) before `/exit`. Cleanly committed; next session picks up -1 with full design context.
- **As uncommitted WC** changes to chores-12 for next session to commit as -1's first action.

I'd lean **commit it now** — cleaner state for `/exit`, no fragile WC across sessions. Would be a quick edit + commit (no cargo cycle needed for pure docs; small Work review pass).

Anything else worth capturing? I don't think so — the gitk/diff context is in backlog #43, and other tooling notes are in CLAUDE.md from the 0.58.0 cycle. But you'll know better.

> DEVELOPER

Definitely commit -0.1 seems resonable, I think we should push too just incase there is a castrophe here, when we restart later we can force-push or maybe smarter is to create a new branch. Although we will not force-push bot it stays linear and on main, I think. Thoughts

> AGENT

You're touching on a real design choice. Three options, each with different shape-flexibility cost:

**A. No interim push.** Local jj op log is the backup. Lowest ceremony, lowest cost. Risk: machine death loses local commits.

**B. Interim push to `main` (FF).** Push `-0` and `-0.1` onto main now. **Cost: force-push at close-out** if we want the merge (or squash) shape — main has already advanced past `0.58.0` to `-0.1`, so the new close-out merge commit replaces those. **Also commits us to "keep" shape** (one commit per sub-step on main) unless we force-push. Loses your 0.59.0 = first-squash goal.

**C. Interim push to feature branch (`feat/0.59.0`).** Create the bookmark, push there. At close-out: merge `feat/0.59.0` into main (FF the merge commit onto `0.58.0`) or squash off the feat tip. **No force-push needed; shape flexibility preserved.** More mechanical steps now; canonical git workflow.

**.claude side regardless:** stays linear on main. Per CLAUDE.md, no parallel `.claude` bookmarks for app-side feature branches. `vc-x1 push` expects ONE bookmark name for both repos, so it might not handle "app=feat, bot=main" out of the box — likely need manual `jj git push` per-repo for the interim. Worth verifying.

**My pick: C.** Preserves the first-squash goal (which is the whole point of 0.59.0). The extra mechanical steps are one-time. Steps:

1. Add design subsections to chores-12 (the `-0.1` work).
2. Commit `-0.1` (app side).
3. `jj bookmark create feat/0.59.0 -r @- -R .` (app side).
4. `jj git push --bookmark feat/0.59.0 -R .` (app side).
5. For .claude safety: `jj commit -R .claude -m "chore: WIP session capture (0.59.0-0.1)" -m "<short body + 2 ochid lines for -0 and -0.1>"` then `jj git push --bookmark main -R .claude`.

At restart:
- `jj git fetch -R .` to sync.
- Continue `-1` on top of `feat/0.59.0`.
- At close-out: `jj rebase -r <0.59.0> -d <0.58.0> -d <-1 tip>` for the merge into main; then `jj git push --bookmark main -R .` (FF). Delete `feat/0.59.0`.

This is more ceremony than B, but B costs us the squash candidacy and a force-push later.

Confirm C (or A/B), and I'll execute through `/exit`.

  "new_string": "## docs: cycle protocol updates (0.59.0)\n\n### Squash carve-out (for CLAUDE.md `### Pushing`)\n\nDefault = merge for any multi-commit cycle. Squash = explicit\nopt-in for cycles whose sub-steps are intermediate validation\npoints rather than distinct landings. Every cycle is ≥3\ncommits (Preparation + ≥1 Work + Close-out), so the\n\"merge of one parent\" edge case is moot.\n\n### Close-out follow-up capture (for CLAUDE.md `### Close-out`)\n\nAt close-out, write any follow-up Todos surfaced during the\ncycle to `notes/todo.md > ## Todo` (if prioritized) or\n`notes/todo-backlog.md` (if long-tail). List them in the\nclose-out commit body so the additions are reviewable.\n\n### Close-out surfaces follow-ups (meta-note, for `## Cycle Protocol`)\n\nClose-out often surfaces follow-up items that became visible\nonly by doing the cycle's work. Capture them at close-out\n(per the rule above) so they survive `/exit` between cycles\n— sessions don't carry conversational context across `/exit`.\n\n# References"
}
